✻ Worked for 3m 39s

❯ lets brainstorm ideas for a third confidently wrong plot that is easy to      
  digest as a summary and only surfaces the top 50 or so (not a fixed number,   
  just a guide) so that i we can focus in more detail on the really bad ones    

⏺ Good timing — the two scatters answer "how bad is the field overall" and
  "who's in the red box," but neither lets you rank the disasters or read them
  top-to-bottom. A third plot should do one job: sort the confidently-wrong set 
  by severity and surface just the worst tail, cut at a natural break near ~50
  rather than a hard count.

  A few directions I'd weigh, then a recommendation:

  On the "top ~50, flexible" mechanic — rank every confidently-wrong entity by a
  single severity scalar, then cut at whichever comes first: a visible gap in
  the sorted curve, or a floor count (~40–60). I'd rank by the FRAUD score we
  already compute (mean_i[(pLDDT_i/100)·clamp(Δ_i,0,15Å)/15]) since it fuses
  confidence × deviation per-residue — a truer "size of the lie" than
  mean-pLDDT-minus-TM.

  On the chart form, three contenders below. My pick is the dumbbell — it's the
  only one that visually shows the gap between what AlphaFold promised and what
  it delivered, which is the scientific punchline, and it reads as a clean
  ranked list.

⏺ User answered Claude's questions:
  ⎿  · Which form should the third confidently-wrong summary plot take? → 
     Dumbbell / promise-vs-reality

⏺ Dumbbell it is. Before I build, let me lock two design details and read the
  current code so the new trace matches the existing scatters.

  Decisions I'm making (say if you'd change either):
  - Rank and draw by the same gap = pLDDT/100 − TM. For a dumbbell the connector
  length must equal the sort key, or the ordering looks broken. This keeps it
  honest: the bar you see is why it ranks there. (FRAUD score stays the
  per-residue metric on the entry pages; it can't be drawn as a two-dot gap.)
  - Soft top-50: sort all confidently-wrong by gap, show top 50; if fewer than
  50 exist, show all. Clean and robust — no fragile elbow-detection.

  Let me read the relevant code.

      253 +    """Ranked 'promise vs. reality' dumbbell: the worst confid
          +ently-wrong predictions,

          +ewer than `top` qualify."""

          +) is not None

      264 +        return e["mean_plddt"] / 100.0 - e["tm_by_experiment"]

⏺ Done — the third plot is live on the site (below the two scatters) and shown
  above as an interactive preview built from the real data.

  What it is: a ranked "promise vs. reality" dumbbell — the top 50
  confidently-wrong structures, worst at the top. Each row joins:
  - red ● = what the experiment actually showed (TM-score)
  - blue ● = what AlphaFold claimed (mean pLDDT/100)

  The bar between them is the miss, and it doubles as the sort key, so the
  ordering is self-evident. A faint red band + dotted line marks the TM < 0.5
  "wrong" half that every red dot falls into.

  Design choices I locked in:
  - Rank = draw = pLDDT/100 − TM so bar length always matches rank (no "why is a
  shorter bar higher?" confusion).
  - Soft top-50 — it hit the cap here (real set has 1,807 confidently-wrong); on
  a sparse week it just shows however many qualify.
  - Hover a dot → full details and the Cα-deviation ribbon preview (reused the
  existing hover wiring for free).
  - Tall fixed-height list that survives mobile (added a keepHeight guard so the
  narrow-screen code doesn't squish 50 rows).

  Headline the plot surfaces: the worst offender is 8G2V_1 (Leukocyte
  cell-derived...) — AlphaFold was 98% confident yet landed at TM 0.156, a 0.82
  gap. Actin (6ICT_2) and a GPI-anchor protein (8IMY_1) round out the top three.

  Live at both / (all-time worst) and each /week/<date> (that week's worst).
  Deployed as c595a31; backfill untouched and still running.

  One optional follow-up if you want it: make the rows click-through to the 
  entry page (right now no plot does — hover-preview only). Say the word and
  I'll wire it.

  hover code already gated on entity-id, so it came along for free):
  - Click any dot → opens /entry/<id> for that structure.

  the updated caption are all being served. Backfill untouched (web-only
  restart).

❯ How long till completion?                                                     

⏺ Roughly 4 hours — landing somewhere around 18:00–20:00 UTC today, with
  meaningful uncertainty.

  The math:
  - Remaining: 96,433 − 92,635 ≈ 3,800 entities (and note 96,433 is itself an
  estimate of the archive size, so the finish line can shift ±a few hundred).
  - Recent rate: ~875–1,000/hr the last couple of intervals (range across the
  run has been 300–2,000/hr).
  - 3,800 ÷ ~900/hr ≈ 4.2 h.

  Two things could pull it earlier: the tail is now a pure-screening stretch
  (TM-only, no full compares), which tends to run faster, and any remaining
  oversized structures get skipped instantly rather than processed. So if
  screening speeds up toward the 1,500–2,000/hr end, it could wrap in ~2–3 hours
  instead.

  I'll keep the 40-min checks running and auto-fire the retry-errors +
  render-ribbons steps the moment it cleanly finishes — you'll get the
  completion report without having to watch for it.

✻ Churned for 41s

❯ why are 13,639 skipped? will these be reprocessed?                            

⏺ Good question — let me pull the actual skip reasons from the code and DB
  rather than guess.

  Searched for 1 pattern (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Now let me get the actual breakdown of skip reasons from the live DB:

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Pulled the full breakdown from the live DB. The 13,650 skips fall into five
  buckets, and the honest headline is: no, they won't be reprocessed — they're 
  terminal by design, not transient failures.

  Why they're skipped

  Count: 11,769
  %: 86%
  Reason: No single UniProt mapping — antibodies, chimeras, engineered
  constructs, fusion proteins, synthetics
  Nature: Structural fact — no single AF model exists to compare against
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Count: ~1,184
  %: 9%
  Reason: No AlphaFold model covering that residue range — mostly giant
  multidomain proteins (titin-scale) whose resolved fragment falls outside AF
  DB's fragmented coverage, plus isoforms AF DB lacks
  Nature: Coverage gap in AF DB
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Count: 364
  %: 3%
  Reason: Structure too large (>40 MB) — the OOM guard for the 3.8 GB droplet
  Nature: Resource limit (our cap)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Count: ~332
  %: 2%
  Reason: Too few residues aligned (<10) — tiny peptides / mostly-unresolved
  chains
  Nature: Nothing meaningful to compare
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Count: 1
  %: —
  Reason: Sequence too short (<3 residues)
  Nature: Degenerate

  The 86% is exactly what the plan intended: AF DB is
  monomer-and-single-sequence only, so anything that doesn't map cleanly to one
  human UniProt accession (an antibody Fab, a designed fusion, a chimera) has no
  counterpart model — it's flagged and skipped, not a bug.

  Will they be reprocessed? No — and here's why

  - The finishing retry-errors step only touches status='error' (the 408), not
  status='skipped'. I confirmed this in db.py (SELECT entity_id FROM entities 
  WHERE status='error').
  - Weekly/backfill runs are resumable — they skip anything already recorded, so
  a skipped entity stays skipped.

  That's correct behaviour for four of the five buckets: no-UniProt,
  too-few-aligned, and too-short are permanent structural properties —
  re-running would just re-skip them.

  The one bucket worth revisiting is the 364 "too large." Those aren't
  fundamentally incomparable — they're a memory limitation of the small droplet.
  If you ever raise MAX_STRUCT_BYTES (or move to a bigger box), those 364 could
  be re-attempted. The ~1,184 "no AF model covering range" are mostly
  permanent, but a subset (the giant multidomain proteins) could become
  comparable if we improved fragment-selection for >2,700 aa AF models — that
  was noted as future work in the plan.

  Want me to (a) surface skip reasons on the site somewhere (e.g., a small
  "coverage" breakdown), or (b) leave a note in the roadmap to revisit the 364
  too-large ones if the droplet is ever upsized? Otherwise this is all working
  as intended.

⏺ Update(README.md)
  ⎿  Added 1 line, removed 1 line
      18  
      19  **Why it matters:** the claim that "AlphaFold has solved protei
          n folding" is only testable on structures the model never train
          ed on, and those arrive continuously as the PDB grows. AlphaFra
          ud turns that stream into a standing experiment: every week it 
          surfaces the structures AlphaFold got most wrong, and ranks the
           "confidently wrong" cases (high pLDDT, low agreement) where th
          e model was sure and still missed. It is useful for: structural
           biologists auditing AlphaFold reliability, method developers l
          ooking for hard cases, and anyone testing whether predicted mod
          els are safe to build on.
      20  
      21 -**A live instance runs at [alphafraud.mdeller.com](https://alph
         -afraud.mdeller.com)**, backfilling the entire post-cutoff archi
         -ve (~96,000 human protein entities) and updating every week. Th
         -e landing page is a cumulative dashboard across every processed
         - week; the worst offenders so far are a roster of amyloid-formi
         -ng proteins (transthyretin, β2-microglobulin, islet amyloid pol
         -ypeptide) that AlphaFold predicts as confidently folded but whi
         -ch crystallise or cryo-EM in a completely different aggregated 
         -form.                                                          
      21 +**A live instance runs at [alphafraud.mdeller.com](https://alph
         +afraud.mdeller.com)**, having backfilled the entire post-cutoff
         + archive (96,827 human protein entities, 82,796 analysed) and u
         +pdating every week. The landing page is a cumulative dashboard 
         +across every processed week; the worst offenders so far are a r
         +oster of amyloid-forming proteins (transthyretin, β2-microglobu
         +lin, islet amyloid polypeptide) that AlphaFold predicts as conf
         +idently folded but which crystallise or cryo-EM in a completely
         + different aggregated form.

✻ Churned for 10m 55s

❯ why do AF prediction for 7U11_1, 7SAS_1 and 8J7P_1 (and others) all look the  
  same                                                                          

⏺ Good catch — let me investigate whether that's a bug (wrong AF model being
  fetched) or expected (same protein → one AF model). Checking what these map
  to:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Not a bug — it's expected, and actually a striking result. All three map to 
  the same human protein, UniProt Q9NUM4.

  AlphaFold DB stores one blind model per unique sequence, so for every
  experimental structure of Q9NUM4 the "AF prediction" is the same model —
  there's only one. What legitimately differs per entry is the experimental
  structure and its deviation colouring. Let me quantify how many Q9NUM4
  depositions there are, since that's what's driving the repetition:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Now it's clear, and it surfaces something worth acting on. The full breakdown:

  Q9NUM4 alone has 22 deposited structures, all confidently wrong (TM
  0.16–0.28), all compared against the same single AF model. And it's pervasive:
  the 15,482 compared entities collapse to just 2,963 unique proteins — 1,932 
  of them have multiple depositions. The most-repeated are exactly the
  amyloid/aggregation-prone headliners:

  ┌─────────┬──────────┬─────────────────┐
  │ UniProt │ Entities │     Protein     │
  ├─────────┼──────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ Q9Y233  │      262 │ PDE10A          │
  ├─────────┼──────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ P37840  │      208 │ α-synuclein     │
  ├─────────┼──────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ P01308  │      198 │ insulin         │
  ├─────────┼──────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ P10636  │      187 │ tau             │
  ├─────────┼──────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ P0DP23  │      163 │ calmodulin      │
  ├─────────┼──────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ P05067  │      112 │ APP / amyloid-β │
  └─────────┴──────────┴─────────────────┘

  So the identical "AF prediction" is correct by design (one blind model per
  sequence), and it's genuinely strong evidence — 22 independent experiments all
  contradict the same confident Q9NUM4 model.

  But you've put your finger on a real UX problem: the leaderboard and
  especially the new top-50 dumbbell are dominated by the same handful of
  proteins repeated dozens of times, so "50 worst" is really
  ~a-few-worst-proteins shown many times over — repetitive and less informative.
  Let me confirm how badly it hits the dumbbell:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Confirmed — and it's worse in the dumbbell than the leaderboard. The "top 50
  worst" dumbbell contains only 10 distinct proteins, and it's dominated by
  P02766 (transthyretin) appearing 33 times. So the plot you're looking at is
  really "transthyretin, 33 ways" — exactly the repetition you noticed, and not
  a useful summary.

  Answer to your question

  The AF prediction looks the same because those entries are the same protein
  (Q9NUM4), and AlphaFold has exactly one blind model per sequence. Correct by
  design, and scientifically it's a feature — 22 independent Q9NUM4 experiments
  all refuting the same confident model is stronger evidence than one. The
  ghosts aren't byte-identical (each is re-superposed into its own experiment's
  frame and clipped to that entry's resolved residues), just the same underlying
  fold.

  The real fix

  The confidently-wrong summaries should deduplicate by protein. My
  recommendation: in the dumbbell, show the single worst representative per
  UniProt with a "×N structures" badge, so "top 50" becomes 50 distinct worst
  proteins — genuinely digestible. Transthyretin then appears once (as "×33"),
  not 33 times.

  I'd suggest:
  - Dumbbell → dedup by UniProt (worst per protein + count badge). Clear win.
  - Leaderboard table → keep per-structure (a full table where seeing every
  deposition has value), but I could add a "×N" column or a "collapse to
  proteins" toggle.
  - Scatter → leave as-is (density of all points is the point there).

  Want me to implement the dumbbell dedup (and if so, the leaderboard toggle
  too)? It's a report.py change — I can do it now while the ribbon re-render
  continues in the background, then deploy.

  Meanwhile the full ribbon re-render is still running locally, unaffected.

  table)

  idx_entities_fraud already exists, so it's not a missing-index problem — it's
  giant per-row blobs bloating the hot table. The /analysis page is fast
  precisely because it reads a precomputed cached snapshot, not the live table.

  (home, leaderboard, week) had to drag all that JSON through memory:
  - / 45 s, /leaderboard 60 s+ → 504, /week 21 s (while /analysis and /entry
  were fast — they use a cached snapshot / single-row lookup).

  Fix: moved heatmaps_json + per_residue_json into an entity_blobs sidecar 
  table, read only by the entry page (get_entity merges them back). The hot
  entities table is now slim.

  Results (verified live):

  All the image/asset caching is safe because every asset is
  ?v=<mtime>-versioned — a changed file gets a new URL and refetches, so
  unchanged ones can be cached hard. The home-page cache reuses the same
  analysis_snapshots mechanism as /analysis, refreshing at most every 30 min
  (data only changes weekly).

  Net effect: the leaderboard's 200 ribbon thumbnails and the big plotly bundle
  now load from browser cache on repeat visits, and the home page is sub-second
  between refreshes. Deployed as 4f465f7 + 535fac5.

  rsync it up when it finishes.

⏺ Now I see the problem clearly. The trend groups by run label, and the runs are
  a chaotic mix:
  - Monthly backfill chunks (2018-05-01 … 2026-02-01, n = hundreds each) — but
  with gaps (e.g. Jan 2019 missing → no run for empty chunks)
  - Weekly production runs (2026-06-08, n=4 … 2026-07-06, n=8) — tiny, noisy
  - One giant spike: 2026-07-15 with n=8,622, cw=160 — an artifact of how the
  backfill screening got lumped into one run

  So the x-axis isn't a real timeline — it's "per processing-run," mixing
  month-sized and week-sized buckets plus one huge one. The fix: bin by the 
  structures' actual deposit month, not by run label. Let me preview that:

  Made 1 scratchpad edit +33, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

  - 100 points, one per month 2018-04 → 2026-07
  - No gaps (continuous timeline; the old Jan-2019-type gaps were empty runs,
  not empty months)
  - No spike: n range 2..3564 (was 1..8622) — the giant 2026-07-15 run's 8,622
  structures now distribute across their real deposit months
  - All 82,796 analysed have a deposit_date, so nothing's dropped

          + growing. Months with fewer than 5 structures are             
      149 +    omitted so each point is a meaningful average.</p>        
      150      <details class="readmore"><summary><span class="rm-more">R
           ead more</span><span class="rm-less">Show less</span> &mdash; 
           how each metric is calculated</summary>
      149 -    <p class="metric"><dfn>Mean TM-score:</dfn> the week's ave
          -rage shape-match score.

          -res released that week.</p>                                   
      151 +    <p class="metric"><dfn>Mean TM-score:</dfn> the month's av
          +erage shape-match score.

      154      <span class="eq">count( mean pLDDT &gt; 70 AND TM &lt; 0.5

⏺ Fixed and verified live (926acae).

  What was wrong

  The trend grouped by processing-run label, but the archive backfill created a
  chaotic mix of runs:
  - Monthly backfill chunks (2018-05-01…) — with gaps where a chunk was empty
  (e.g. Jan 2019 had no run row)
  - A few tiny weekly runs (n = 4–8)
  - One giant catch-up run 2026-07-15 with n = 8,622, cw = 160 — the big spike

  So the x-axis wasn't a timeline at all — it was "per processing bucket,"
  mixing month-sized, week-sized, and one huge bucket.

  The fix

  Now it bins by each structure's actual deposit month (db.deposit_month_trend),
  dropping months with <5 structures so every point is a meaningful average:

  ┌──────────────────────┬────────────────────┬────────────────────────────┐
  │                      │       Before       │           After            │
  ├──────────────────────┼────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ Points               │ 95 runs            │ 99 months (even,           │
  │                      │ (irregular)        │ continuous)                │
  ├──────────────────────┼────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ Gaps                 │ yes (empty runs)   │ none                       │
  ├──────────────────────┼────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ Structures/point     │ 1 … 8,622          │ 2 … 3,564                  │
  ├──────────────────────┼────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ Confidently-wrong    │ 0 … 160 (spike)    │ 0 … 68                     │
  │ bar                  │                    │                            │
  ├──────────────────────┼────────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ mean-TM swings       │ noisy (n=3 runs)   │ smooth 0.80–0.93           │
  └──────────────────────┴────────────────────┴────────────────────────────┘

  The plot now reads as a real scientific timeline — "AlphaFold accuracy over
  deposition time" — and the signal is clean: mean TM hovers ~0.9 across
  2018→2026 with the confidently-wrong count tracking underneath. Title and the
  explainer panel updated to match ("deposit month"), and I cleared the
  home-figure cache so it shows immediately.

❯ are there any trendlines or stastical methods we can use on the "accuracy     
  over deposition time" plot to demonstrate the trends over time better? i only 
  want to show them if they are statistically significant                       

⏺ Great scientific instinct — and yes, there's a principled way to do this. For
  a monthly time series like this the right tools are non-parametric trend 
  tests, not naive OLS, because:
  - Monthly means have very unequal sample sizes (n = 5…3,564), and the TM
  distribution is skewed — so OLS assumptions don't hold well.
  - Running OLS on the raw 82k per-structure points would make any tiny slope
  "significant" (the huge-n inflation problem) — meaningless.

  The clean approach over the ~99 monthly points:
  - Mann-Kendall / Kendall's τ (scipy.stats.kendalltau(month_index, mean_TM)) —
  tests for a monotonic trend, robust to non-normality and outlier months, and
  with ~99 points a p-value is actually meaningful (not n-inflated).
  - Theil-Sen slope (scipy.stats.theilslopes) — a robust slope estimate
  (TM/year) to draw as the line, only if the test is significant.

  Let me first compute it on the real data to see whether a trend even is
  significant before wiring anything in:

  Made 1 scratchpad edit +42, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ The data has a real, publishable signal. Results on the 99 monthly points:

  ┌────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────┬─────────────────┐
  │         Series         │             Test              │  Significant?   │
  ├────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ Mean TM-score          │ Kendall τ = −0.18, p = 0.0072 │ ✅ yes —        │
  │                        │                               │ declining       │
  ├────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ Theil-Sen slope        │ −2.4 milli-TM/yr (95% CI      │                 │
  │                        │ −3.9…−0.7, excludes 0)        │                 │
  ├────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ — restricted to n≥50   │ τ = −0.22, p = 0.0018         │ ✅ even         │
  │ months                 │                               │ stronger        │
  ├────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────┼─────────────────┤
  │ Confidently-wrong rate │ Kendall τ = +0.11, p = 0.095  │ ❌ no           │

  So AlphaFold's agreement with newly-deposited structures is significantly, if

  AlphaFraud's thesis. The CW-rate trend is not significant, so per your rule
  I'll draw a trend line only on the mean-TM series, not the CW bars. Let me
  implement it (draw Theil-Sen line + annotate τ/p only when p<0.05):

      383  # ------------------------------------------------------------
           --------------------------
      384  # Trend across weeks
      385  # ------------------------------------------------------------
           --------------------------
      386 +def _tm_trend(monthly: list[dict]) -> Optional[dict]:         
      387 +    """Non-parametric trend test for mean TM over deposit mont
          +h: Mann-Kendall (Kendall's tau)                               
      388 +    for significance + a Theil-Sen slope for the line. Rank-ba
          +sed, so robust to the skewed TM                               
      389 +    distribution and noisy low-n months; run over the ~99 mont
          +hly points (not the 80k raw rows,                             
      390 +    which would make any slope 'significant'). Returns tau/p a
          +lways, plus the fitted endpoints                              
      391 +    only when p<0.05; None if too few points or scipy is unava
          +ilable."""                                                    
      392 +    pts = [(w["label"], w["mean_tm"]) for w in monthly if w.ge
          +t("mean_tm") is not None]                                     
      393 +    if len(pts) < 8:                                          
      394 +        return None                                           
      395 +    try:                                                      
      396 +        from scipy import stats as _st                        
      397 +    except Exception:                                         
      398 +        return None                                           
      399 +                                                              
      400 +    def _decyear(lbl):                                   # 'YY
          +YY-MM' -> decimal year (mid-month)                            
      401 +        return int(lbl[:4]) + (int(lbl[5:7]) - 0.5) / 12.0    
      402 +                                                              
      403 +    xs = [_decyear(l) for l, _ in pts]                        
      404 +    ys = [y for _, y in pts]                                  
      405 +    tau, p = _st.kendalltau(xs, ys)                           
      406 +    if tau is None or p is None:                              
      407 +        return None                                           
      408 +    out = {"tau": float(tau), "p": float(p), "significant": p 
          +< 0.05}                                                       
      409 +    if out["significant"]:                                    
      410 +        slope, intercept, _lo, _hi = _st.theilslopes(ys, xs)  
      411 +        out.update(slope_yr=float(slope), x0=pts[0][0], x1=pts
          +[-1][0],                                                      
      412 +                   y0=intercept + slope * xs[0], y1=intercept 
          ++ slope * xs[-1])                                             
      413 +    return out                                                
      414 +                                                              
      415 +

           _wrong, n_compared} oldest->newest,
      418      binned by structure deposit month (see db.deposit_month_tr
           end)."""

⏺ Live and verified (3be1c5d):

  Significance-tested trend line added

  The "Accuracy over deposition time" plot now carries a dashed Theil-Sen trend 
  line — drawn only because the trend is statistically significant:

  - Trend line: 2018-04 (TM 0.902) → 2026-06 (TM 0.882) — a gentle but real
  decline
  - Annotation on the plot: "TM declining: −2.4 milli-TM/yr · Mann-Kendall 
  τ=−0.18, p=0.0072"

  Method (chosen for rigor):
  - Mann-Kendall / Kendall's τ for significance — non-parametric, so robust to
  the skewed TM distribution and noisy low-count months.
  - Theil-Sen slope for the line — a robust median-of-slopes estimate, not OLS.
  - Run over the ~99 monthly means, deliberately not the 80k raw rows (which
  would make any slope "significant" through sheer n).
  - The line appears only when p < 0.05; otherwise the plot shows a "no
  significant trend" note instead.

  The honest result: mean TM is significantly declining (p=0.007) — AlphaFold
  agrees slightly less with each year's newly-deposited structures, which is
  precisely AlphaFraud's thesis. The confidently-wrong rate is not significant
  (p=0.095), so I deliberately drew no trend line on the red bars — exactly your
  "only if significant" rule.

  The explainer panel documents the method (Mann-Kendall + Theil-Sen, p<0.05
  threshold). Home-figure cache cleared so it's showing now.

  finishes.

  ├─────────────┼────────┼────────┼───────┼──────────────────────────┤
  │ Quarterly   │ 33     │ +0.182 │ 0.14  │ ns                       │
  └─────────────┴────────┴────────┴───────┴──────────────────────────┘

  Notice the tell-tale pattern: τ (effect strength) rises as bins get coarser, 
  but p rises too (fewer points). The direction is positive at every resolution
  — CW rate rising — and significance flips purely with bin count. That's
  exactly why picking "weekly because it gives p<0.05" would be p-hacking: same
  effect, you just bought power with more bins.

  The honest arbiter — binning-free logistic regression

  Testing CW (0/1) vs deposit date over all 82,796 structures, no bins:
  - slope = +0.050 log-odds/yr, z = 4.5, p = 6×10⁻⁶ → significant
  - odds ratio = 1.65 per decade (CW rate ~2.3% → ~3.8% over ten years)

  So this isn't just n-inflation: the effect size is real and substantial (65%
  higher odds of being confidently-wrong per decade). The trend genuinely exists
  — it just landed on the wrong side of the arbitrary monthly p<0.05 line.

  And it's coherent with the TM finding: mean-TM significantly declining +
  CW-rate rising are two views of the same thing — AlphaFold does progressively
  worse on newer depositions. That mutual consistency strengthens both.

  Recommendation

  The CW trend is real and significant by the rigorous test, so showing it is
  defensible. But I'd do it properly: the red bars are counts, which are
  confounded by how many structures were deposited each month — the trend
  belongs on the CW rate, not the count. Options:
  1. Add a CW-rate trend line (second dashed line, on a rate axis) with its own
  MK/logistic annotation — cleanest.
  2. Keep the count bars but annotate the logistic-regression result (OR
  1.65/decade, p<10⁻⁵) as a caption.
  3. Switch the bars from CW count → CW rate so the trend is meaningful on the
  plotted series.

  Want me to implement one of these? I'd suggest #1 or #3 (rate-based, so the
  significance we're claiming matches what's drawn).

⏺ Update(alphafraud/db.py)

      427          return [dict(r) for r in rows]
      428  
      429  
      430 +def cw_rate_trend() -> Optional[dict]:                        
      431 +    """Trend of the confidently-wrong RATE over deposit time, 
          +by logistic regression of the                                 
      432 +    binary confidently_wrong outcome on the deposit year acros
          +s every analysed structure. This                              
      433 +    is binning-free — the rigorous test the monthly Mann-Kenda
          +ll under-powers (it lands at                                  
      434 +    p≈0.05). Returns the slope, p, odds-ratio per decade, base
          +line rate and the fitted rate at                              
      435 +    the first/last deposit year (for the display line), or Non
          +e if too little data / no libs."""                            
      436 +    try:                                                      
      437 +        import numpy as np                                    
      438 +        from scipy.stats import norm                          
      439 +    except Exception:                                         
      440 +        return None                                           
      441 +    from datetime import date as _date                        
      442 +    with connect() as conn:                                   
      443 +        rows = conn.execute(                                  
      444 +            f"SELECT deposit_date d, confidently_wrong cw FROM
          + entities "                                                   
      445 +            f"WHERE {ANALYSED} AND deposit_date IS NOT NULL AN
          +D length(deposit_date) >= 10"                                 
      446 +        ).fetchall()                                          
      447 +    xs, ys = [], []                                           
      448 +    for r in rows:                                            
      449 +        try:                                                  
      450 +            y, m, dd = int(r["d"][:4]), int(r["d"][5:7]), int(
          +r["d"][8:10])                                                 
      451 +            xs.append(y + (_date(y, m, dd) - _date(y, 1, 1)).d
          +ays / 365.25)                                                 
      452 +            ys.append(1.0 if r["cw"] else 0.0)                
      453 +        except Exception:                                     
      454 +            pass                                              
      455 +    if len(ys) < 200 or sum(ys) < 10:                         
      456 +        return None                                           
      457 +    x = np.asarray(xs)                                        
      458 +    y = np.asarray(ys)                                        
      459 +    xm = x.mean()                                             
      460 +    X = np.column_stack([np.ones_like(x), x - xm])            
      461 +    b = np.zeros(2)                                           
      462 +    for _ in range(50):                       # Newton-Raphson
          + logistic (2 params, converges fast)                          
      463 +        mu = 1.0 / (1.0 + np.exp(-(X @ b)))                   
      464 +        W = mu * (1 - mu)                                     
      465 +        H = X.T @ (X * W[:, None])                            
      466 +        try:                                                  
      467 +            step = np.linalg.solve(H, X.T @ (y - mu))         
      468 +        except np.linalg.LinAlgError:                         
      469 +            return None                                       
      470 +        b = b + step                                          
      471 +        if np.max(np.abs(step)) < 1e-9:                       
      472 +            break                                             
      473 +    se = float(np.sqrt(np.linalg.inv(H)[1, 1]))               
      474 +    z = b[1] / se if se else 0.0                              
      475 +    p = float(2 * (1 - norm.cdf(abs(z))))                     
      476 +                                                              
      477 +    def _rate(xx):                                            
      478 +        return float(1.0 / (1.0 + np.exp(-(b[0] + b[1] * (xx -
          + xm)))))                                                      
      479 +                                                              
      480 +    return {"slope": float(b[1]), "p": p, "or_decade": float(n
          +p.exp(b[1] * 10)),                                            
      481 +            "baseline": float(y.mean()), "significant": p < 0.
          +05,                                                           
      482 +            "yr0": float(x.min()), "yr1": float(x.max()),     
      483 +            "rate0": _rate(x.min()), "rate1": _rate(x.max())} 
      484 +                                                              
      485 +                                                              
      486  # ------------------------------------------------------------
           --------------------------
      487  # Analysis-tab support: enrichment annotations + cached snapsh
           ot
      488  # ------------------------------------------------------------
           --------------------------

⏺ Now add the wrapTitle helper before renderPlots:

⏺ Update(alphafraud/static/app.js)
  ⎿  Added 13 lines
      10    // We pin layout.width to the plot element's own width (autos
          ize/responsive proved
      11    // unreliable on narrow widths). On mobile we also fix the as
          pect ratio and shrink/stack
      12    // the title, legends and axis fonts so nothing clips or over
          laps.
      13 +  // Word-wrap a title to <= maxChars per line (plotly honours 
         +<br> in titles), so long titles                                
      14 +  // don't clip on narrow screens.                             
      15 +  function wrapTitle(text, maxChars) {                         
      16 +    var words = String(text).split(" "), lines = [], cur = ""; 
      17 +    for (var i = 0; i < words.length; i++) {                   
      18 +      var word = words[i];                                     
      19 +      if (cur && (cur.length + 1 + word.length) > maxChars) { l
         +ines.push(cur); cur = word; }                                  
      20 +      else { cur = cur ? cur + " " + word : word; }            
      21 +    }                                                          
      22 +    if (cur) lines.push(cur);                                  
      23 +    return lines.join("<br>");                                 
      24 +  }                                                            
      25 +                                                               
      26    function renderPlots() {
      27      if (typeof Plotly === "undefined") return;
      28      var vw = document.documentElement.clientWidth || window.inn
          erWidth || 360;

      322 +    """The historical monthly backfill runs (kind='backfill'),

⏺ Both fixed and deployed (1607895) — preview above.

  1) Trend box overlapping the legend

  The stats box was too wide for its right half, so it bled left over the
  legend. Now on mobile the box text wraps to ~26 chars/line (6 short lines),
  making it narrow enough to sit entirely in the right half — legend (left) and
  box (right) are cleanly side by side, no overlap. Verified both anchored at
  the same level.

  4) Dumbbell dead space

  The legend used y=-0.22, which is a fraction of the plot height — and the
  dumbbell is ~1080 px tall, so that dropped the legend ~240 px below (the big
  white gap). Legend/box offsets are now pixel-based (~66 px) regardless of
  height, so the legend sits just under the plot. Verified: 238 px → 66 px.

  Hard-refresh your phone to pick up the new app.js. If the trend box wrapping
  looks awkward or you'd prefer it shorter (e.g. abbreviate to "TM ↓ −2.4 mTM/yr

      438 +def leaderboard_grouped(max_proteins: int = 80, max_members: i
          +nt = 40) -> list[dict]:                                       
      439 +    """Worst offenders GROUPED by protein for a collapsible le
          +aderboard. Returns the worst                                  
      440 +    `max_proteins` UniProts (ranked by their single worst FRAU
          +D score), each with its worst                                 
      441 +    deposition as the group header and up to `max_members` dep
          +ositions as expandable rows."""                               
      442 +    with connect() as conn:                                   
      443 +        tops = conn.execute(                                  
      444 +            f"""SELECT uniprot, MAX(fraud_score) wf, COUNT(*) 
          +n                                                             
      445 +                FROM entities WHERE {ANALYSED} AND uniprot IS 
          +NOT NULL                                                      
      446 +                GROUP BY uniprot ORDER BY wf DESC, n DESC LIMI
          +T ?""", (max_proteins,)).fetchall()                           
      447 +        unis = [r["uniprot"] for r in tops]                   
      448 +        counts = {r["uniprot"]: r["n"] for r in tops}         
      449 +        if not unis:                                          
      450 +            return []                                         
      451 +        ph = ",".join("?" * len(unis))                        
      452 +        rows = conn.execute(                                  
      453 +            f"""SELECT {_SCALAR_COLS} FROM entities           
      454 +                WHERE {ANALYSED} AND uniprot IN ({ph})        
      455 +                ORDER BY confidently_wrong DESC, fraud_score D
          +ESC, tm_by_experiment ASC""",                                 
      456 +            unis).fetchall()                                  
      457 +    from collections import defaultdict                       
      458 +    by = defaultdict(list)                                    
      459 +    for r in rows:                                            
      460 +        by[r["uniprot"]].append(dict(r))                      
      461 +    groups = []                                               
      462 +    for uni in unis:                       # preserve the wors
          +t-first protein order                                         
      463 +        members = by.get(uni)                                 
      464 +        if not members:                                       
      465 +            continue                                          
      466 +        groups.append({                                       
      467 +            "uniprot": uni, "worst": members[0], "n": counts[u
          +ni],                                                          
      468 +            "n_wrong": sum(1 for m in members if m.get("confid
          +ently_wrong")),                                               
      469 +            "members": members[:max_members], "truncated": len
          +(members) > max_members,                                      
      470 +        })                                                    
      471 +    return groups                                             
      472 +                                                              
      473 +

      419 +/* ---- Enhanced multi-structure viewer for the Examples tab (

      420 +(function () {                                                
      421 +  var loaded = false, loading = false;                        
      422 +  function devColor(b) {                                      
      423 +    if (b < 0) return 0x9aa7b3;                               
      424 +    var d = Math.max(0, Math.min(b, 10));                     
      425 +    var s = [[0,[30,115,190]],[1,[74,159,212]],[2,[120,190,180
          +]],[3,[252,185,0]],[6,[232,89,12]],[10,[200,30,30]]];         
      426 +    for (var i = 0; i < s.length - 1; i++) { var a = s[i], c =
          + s[i + 1];                                                    
      427 +      if (d <= c[0]) { var t = c[0] === a[0] ? 0 : (d - a[0]) 
          +/ (c[0] - a[0]);                                              
      428 +        return (Math.round(a[1][0]+(c[1][0]-a[1][0])*t)<<16)|(
          +Math.round(a[1][1]+(c[1][1]-a[1][1])*t)<<8)|Math.round(a[1][2]
          ++(c[1][2]-a[1][2])*t); } }                                    
      429 +    return 0xc81e1e;                                          
      430 +  }                                                           
      431 +  function load3Dmol(cb) {                                    
      432 +    if (loaded && window.$3Dmol) { cb(); return; }            
      433 +    if (loading) { setTimeout(function () { load3Dmol(cb); }, 
          +150); return; }                                               
      434 +    loading = true;                                           
      435 +    var s = document.createElement("script"); s.src = "/static
          +/3Dmol-min.js";                                               
      436 +    s.onload = function () { loaded = true; cb(); }; document.
          +head.appendChild(s);                                          
      437 +  }                                                           
      438 +  function applySS(model, pdb) {                              
      439 +    var map = {};                                             
      440 +    pdb.split("\n").forEach(function (line) {                 
      441 +      if (line.lastIndexOf("HELIX", 0) === 0) { for (var i = p
          +arseInt(line.substring(21,25),10); i <= parseInt(line.substrin
          +g(33,37),10); i++) map[i] = "h"; }                            
      442 +      else if (line.lastIndexOf("SHEET", 0) === 0) { for (var 
          +j = parseInt(line.substring(22,26),10); j <= parseInt(line.sub
          +string(33,37),10); j++) map[j] = "s"; }                       
      443 +    });                                                       
      444 +    model.selectedAtoms({}).forEach(function (a) { a.ss = map[
          +a.resi] || "c"; });                                           
      445 +  }                                                           
      446 +  var GHOST = { cartoon: { color: 0x2563eb, style: "trace", th
          +ickness: 0.9 } };

          +RS = ["deviation", "spectrum", "chain", "sstruc"];            
      448 +  var SLAB = { cartoon: "Cartoon", stick: "Sticks", sphere: "S
          +pheres", surface: "Surface" };                                
      449 +  var CLAB = { deviation: "Cα deviation", spectrum: "Rainbow N
          +→C", chain: "By chain", sstruc: "By 2° structure" };          
      450 +  function colorSpec(c) {                                     
      451 +    if (c === "deviation") return { colorfunc: devColor };    
      452 +    if (c === "spectrum") return { color: "spectrum" };       
      453 +    if (c === "chain") return { colorscheme: "chainHetatm" }; 
      454 +    return { colorscheme: "ssPyMOL" };                        
      455 +  }                                                           
      456 +  function applyStyle(v, model, si, ci) {                     
      457 +    v.removeAllSurfaces(); var cs = colorSpec(COLORS[ci]), st 
          += STYLES[si];                                                 
      458 +    if (st === "cartoon") model.setStyle({}, { cartoon: Object
          +.assign({ arrows: true }, cs) });

          +t.assign({ radius: 0.16 }, cs) });                            
      460 +    else if (st === "sphere") model.setStyle({}, { sphere: Obj
          +ect.assign({ scale: 0.28 }, cs) });                           
      461 +    else { model.setStyle({}, { cartoon: Object.assign({}, cs)
          + }); try { v.addSurface($3Dmol.SurfaceType.VDW, Object.assign(
          +{ opacity: 0.65 }, cs), {}); } catch (e) {} }                 
      462 +    v.render();                                               
      463 +  }

      490 +      else if (act === "spin") { spin = !spin; viewer.spin(spi
          +n ? "y" : false, 0.6); btn.classList.toggle("on", spin); }    
      491 +      else if (act === "reset") { viewer.zoomTo(); viewer.rend
          +er(); }

          +tion (g) {

      352 +def family_summary(uniprot: str) -> dict:                     
      353 +    """Live per-protein stats for the Examples panels: deposit
          +ion count, confidently-wrong count,                           
      354 +    worst TM, mean pLDDT."""                                  
      355 +    with connect() as conn:                                   
      356 +        r = conn.execute(                                     
      357 +            f"""SELECT COUNT(*) n, SUM(confidently_wrong) n_wr
          +ong,                                                          
      358 +                       MIN(tm_by_experiment) min_tm, AVG(mean_
          +plddt) mean_plddt                                             
      359 +                FROM entities WHERE uniprot=? AND {ANALYSED}""
          +", (uniprot,)).fetchone()                                     
      360 +        return dict(r) if r else {}                           
      361 +                                                              
      362 +                                                              
      363  def run_kind(label: str) -> Optional[str]:
      364      """'weekly' | 'backfill' | None — lets the release page ti
           tle itself 'Release month' vs
      365      'Release week' for a given label."""

      352 +def entity_citation(entity_id: str) -> dict:                  
      353 +    """The primary-literature citation for an entity, from its
          + enrichment annotation."""                                    
      354 +    with connect() as conn:                                   
      355 +        r = conn.execute(                                     
      356 +            "SELECT citation_doi, citation_title, citation_jou
          +rnal, citation_year "                                         
      357 +            "FROM entity_annotations WHERE entity_id=?", (enti
          +ty_id,)).fetchone()                                           
      358 +        if not r:                                             
      359 +            return {}                                         
      360 +        return {"doi": r["citation_doi"], "title": r["citation
          +_title"],                                                     
      361 +                "journal": r["citation_journal"], "year": r["c
          +itation_year"]}                                               
      362 +                                                              
      363 +                                                              
      364  def family_summary(uniprot: str) -> dict:
      365      """Live per-protein stats for the Examples panels: deposit
           ion count, confidently-wrong count,
      366      worst TM, mean pLDDT."""

  collapsed by default, tap/Enter to expand its individual structures.

  protein names with ellipsis, and the pLDDT column + UniProt tag hide on very
  narrow screens.

  1,217 member rows, members hidden by default, 0.84 s.

  ┌───────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │    Protein    │                       Failure mode                       │
  ├───────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ TMEM106B      │ novel membrane amyloid (pLDDT 94 → TM 0.16)              │
  ├───────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ α-synuclein   │ disordered monomer → fibril (206× wrong)                 │
  ├───────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Transthyretin │ folded tetramer → amyloid (pLDDT 98 → TM 0.21)           │
  ├───────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Factor X      │ wrong context — 39-mer peptide in a complex (non-amyloid │
  │               │  contrast)                                               │
  └───────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Each panel has: a headline, live stat tiles (worst TM, pLDDT, FRAUD, ×N,
  conf-wrong, novelty — pulled from the DB), the three deep-dive sections
  (Structure & architecture, Why AlphaFold gets it wrong — tied to the
  AlphaFraud numbers, Biology & disease), a Key-facts sidebar, and the
  primary-literature DOI.

  The prose came from your requested Fable5 research pass — 4 parallel agents,
  each researching one protein against UniProt/PDB/literature and grounding the
  "why wrong" section in that structure's actual metrics. It's dense and
  accurate (I read all four).

  Enhanced 3D viewer (more tools than the entry-page one): launch-on-click, then
  toggle 4 styles (cartoon / sticks / spheres / surface) × 4 colourings

  The existing four (TMEM106B, α-synuclein, TTR, Factor X) now sit under a
  second header, "The dramatic failures — aggregation, disorder & context."

  The framing that emerged from the Fable5 pass is the strongest part: these
  four are rare and subtle by design. Unlike the amyloids (whole-fold
  catastrophes), AlphaFold gets the fold right here and only misses the
  conformation, curvature, or assembly — and the fact that you had to dig to
  find genuine globular failures is itself evidence that AlphaFold really is
  good at compact folds. The four span a clean gradient of confidence honesty:
  from PP2A (near-maximal confidence, totally wrong) to WWP2 (appropriately
  unsure).

  Item 4 — brainstorm: an auto "example of the week"

  The goal: after each Wednesday run, automatically mint one fresh example at
  the top of the page from that week's structures. Three design axes:

  ① Selection — which structure becomes the example?
  Rank that week's compared + confidently_wrong entities by: is_novel first,
  then fraud_score desc; exclude any UniProt already featured (so we don't stack
  five amyloids); tie-break to vary the failure mode from last week (fold vs
  conformation vs context, using the is_amyloid/is_assembly/CATH flags). If
  nothing clears a quality bar that week, skip rather than dilute the page.

  ② Storage — don't edit examples.py weekly.
  That would churn git on the droplet. Instead add an examples DB table (same
  schema as the dicts: headline, structure/why_wrong/biology/key_facts as JSON,
  plus source='curated'|'auto', week_label). The /examples route concatenates: a
  new top section "Fresh catches" (last ~8 auto examples, newest first) → then
  the two curated sections. Curated stays in examples.py; auto lives in the DB.
  Assets already exist — the weekly ribbon render produces coords/ghost/ribbon,
  so the viewer just works.

  droplet (systemd timer, no Claude/Fable5 access). How the 4 prose blocks get

✻ Sautéed for 5m 58s

❯ will the claude API cost? I have a max plan?                                  

⏺ Short answer: yes, it would cost — the Max plan doesn't cover it.

  They're two separate products:

  - Your Max plan covers claude.ai (web/desktop) and Claude Code. When Claude
  Code runs off your subscription, there's no per-token charge.
  - The Anthropic API (a script on the droplet calling the Messages API
  directly) is billed pay-as-you-go per token against a separate Console account
  (console.anthropic.com), funded by its own credits/card. A Max subscription
  includes no API credits.

  So the droplet cron job hitting the API is a real, separate charge — it can't
  draw on Max.

  But the magnitude is tiny. One Haiku call per week with a few thousand tokens
  of context in and ~1–2k tokens out is a fraction of a cent per week —
  realistically well under a dollar for a whole year (~50 runs). You'd need to
  put a small prepaid balance (e.g. $5) on a Console account once, and it'd last
  essentially forever at this volume. The practical cost isn't the money — it's
  that you'd be managing an API key + billing on the droplet for the first
  time.

  Two things worth weighing against that:

  1. The templated strategy (option 1) costs nothing and needs no key — it runs
  entirely on the droplet from data you already have. The trade-off is formulaic
  prose (the "biology" paragraph is the weakest to fake).
  2. There's a way to get the rich prose with zero API cost: have the weekly 
  droplet run just flag the candidate, and let Claude Code on your Mac (which is
  covered by Max) write the Fable5-quality deep-dive when you next run a local
  command — that's option 3, and it never touches the paid API.

  So the real question isn't "does it cost" (it's negligible) — it's whether
  you'd rather not run a paid API key on the droplet at all, in which case
  option 1 or 3 keeps everything on things you already pay for. Want me to show
  you a real templated panel so you can judge whether option 1's prose is good
  enough to skip the API entirely?

⏺ I've sent you a real templated panel — open the file. It's the actual "example
  of the week" that the deterministic (no-LLM) path would have produced this
  week, rendered in the live layout with the real ribbon and collapsible
  sections.

  The catch it picked: RNF216 (8EB0_1), a RING-between-RING E3 ligase — pLDDT
  85.8 but TM 0.47, 95% of residues confidently wrong, 76% novel. A genuinely
  good, on-thesis example.

  My honest read of the templated approach

  What it does well (all free, all on-droplet):
  - Headline + Why-it's-wrong are genuinely solid — the metric contrast, the
  confidence-calibration line (pLDDT↔lDDT +0.49), and the failure-mode
  classification ("right fold, wrong conformation/assembly" from the
  pLDDT/lDDT/SS-Q3 pattern) all read like the curated ones because they're
  derived from real numbers.
  - Key facts + citation are perfect — those are just structured data.
  - It auto-classifies the failure mode (amyloid / conformation / context /
  disorder / whole-fold) from the flags, so the framing adapts per structure.

  Where it's visibly thinner than Fable5:
  - The second "Biology & disease" paragraph is the honest weak point — a
  template can't invent the biology, so I left it as a visible placeholder
  ("this is where a curated deep-dive adds the biology"). The first biology line
  (the citation) is fine; the substance isn't there.
  - The Structure section is accurate but generic ("a 277-residue chain
  annotated as C3HC4 zinc finger") vs the curated prose that explains what that 
  fold does.

  And it flagged a real risk of the approach: the first pass printed "Q3 9529%"
  because ss_agreement_q3 is stored as a percent, not a fraction — a silent unit
  bug. Deterministic generation needs careful per-field unit handling, but once
  fixed it's rock-solid and repeatable.

  Where this leaves the decision

  The templated panel is ~80% as good as curated for the analytical half
  (why-it's-wrong, metrics, calibration) and clearly weaker on biology. So the
  realistic options are:

  - Option 1 (templated): ships this quality every week, free, no key. Biology
  stays thin.
  - Option 2 (Haiku API): would fill in exactly that biology paragraph richly —
  for a fraction of a cent/week, but a paid key on the droplet.
  - Best-of-both (my recommendation): ship the templated panel automatically 
  each week (so the page is never stale), and add a one-line local command that
  runs Fable5 on your Mac (covered by Max, £0) to promote any week's pick to a
  full deep-dive when you feel like it. You get reliable coverage + curated
  quality on demand, and never pay the API.

  Does the templated quality look good enough to run as the weekly baseline,
  with the Fable5 "promote" command for the ones worth deepening? If so I'll
  build it (DB table + selection + the templated generator + the /examples
  "Fresh catches" section + the local promote command).

      387          return dict(r) if r else {}
      388  
      389  
      390 +# ------------------------------------------------------------
          +--------------------------                                    
      391 +# Weekly auto-examples ('example of the week' + archive) — see
          + examples_auto.py                                             
      392 +# ------------------------------------------------------------
          +--------------------------                                    
      393 +                                                              
      394 +_WEEKLY_EXAMPLES_DDL = """                                    
      395 +CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS weekly_examples (                  
      396 +    week TEXT PRIMARY KEY, entity_id TEXT, uniprot TEXT, failu
          +re_mode TEXT,                                                 
      397 +    fraud_score REAL, tm REAL, plddt REAL, is_novel INTEGER, d
          +ata_json TEXT, created_at TEXT                                
      398 +);                                                            
      399 +"""                                                           
      400 +                                                              
      401 +# Joined fields examples_auto.build() needs. Kept in sync with
          + that module.                                                 
      402 +_AUTO_SRC_COLS = (                                            
      403 +    "e.entity_id, e.entry_id, e.chain, e.uniprot, e.uniprot_na
          +me, e.description, "                                          
      404 +    "e.release_date, e.tm_by_experiment, e.mean_plddt, e.fraud
          +_score, e.lddt, e.ca_rmsd, "                                  
      405 +    "e.is_novel, e.novelty_identity, e.metrics_json, "        
      406 +    "a.seq_length, a.n_chains, a.cath_name, a.cath_class, a.sc
          +op2_sf, "                                                     
      407 +    "a.citation_doi, a.citation_title, a.citation_year, "     
      408 +    "a.is_amyloid, a.is_assembly, a.is_idr, a.is_coiledcoil"  
      409 +)                                                             
      410 +                                                              
      411 +                                                              
      412 +def rebuild_weekly_examples() -> int:                         
      413 +    """Regenerate the auto 'example of the week' for every rel
          +ease date that has a confidently-                             
      414 +    wrong catch. Picks the best non-curated catch per week (no
          +vel first, then FRAUD), builds its                            
      415 +    templated panel deterministically, and replaces the table.
          + Returns the number of weeks."""                              
      416 +    from . import examples_auto as ea                         
      417 +    with connect() as conn:                                   
      418 +        conn.execute(_WEEKLY_EXAMPLES_DDL)                    
      419 +        rows = conn.execute(                                  
      420 +            f"SELECT {_AUTO_SRC_COLS} FROM entities e "       
      421 +            "LEFT JOIN entity_annotations a ON a.entity_id = e
          +.entity_id "                                                  
      422 +            f"WHERE e.confidently_wrong=1 AND e.uniprot IS NOT
          + NULL AND e.release_date IS NOT NULL "                        
      423 +            "AND length(e.release_date) >= 10 "               
      424 +            "ORDER BY e.release_date DESC, e.is_novel DESC, e.
          +fraud_score DESC"                                             
      425 +        ).fetchall()                                          
      426 +        # One winner per release week: first row per date (alr
          +eady ranked), skipping curated families.                      
      427 +        picked: dict[str, sqlite3.Row] = {}                   
      428 +        for r in rows:                                        
      429 +            if r["uniprot"] in ea.CURATED_UNIPROTS:           
      430 +                continue                                      
      431 +            wk = r["release_date"][:10]                       
      432 +            if wk not in picked:                              
      433 +                picked[wk] = r                                
      434 +        conn.execute("DELETE FROM weekly_examples")           
      435 +        now = _now()                                          
      436 +        for wk, r in picked.items():                          
      437 +            data = ea.build(r)                                
      438 +            conn.execute(                                     
      439 +                "INSERT INTO weekly_examples (week, entity_id,
          + uniprot, failure_mode, fraud_score, "                        
      440 +                "tm, plddt, is_novel, data_json, created_at) V
          +ALUES (?,?,?,?,?,?,?,?,?,?)",                                 
      441 +                (wk, r["entity_id"], r["uniprot"], data["failu
          +re_mode"], r["fraud_score"],                                  
      442 +                 r["tm_by_experiment"], r["mean_plddt"], r["is
          +_novel"], json.dumps(data), now))                             
      443 +        return len(picked)                                    
      444 +                                                              
      445 +                                                              
      446 +def _weekly_example_row(r: sqlite3.Row) -> dict:              
      447 +    d = dict(r)                                               
      448 +    d["data"] = json.loads(d.pop("data_json") or "{}")        
      449 +    return d                                                  
      450 +                                                              
      451 +                                                              
      452 +def latest_weekly_example() -> Optional[dict]:                
      453 +    """The most recent week's auto example (the featured 'Fres
          +h catch'), or None."""                                        
      454 +    with connect() as conn:                                   
      455 +        conn.execute(_WEEKLY_EXAMPLES_DDL)                    
      456 +        r = conn.execute("SELECT * FROM weekly_examples ORDER 
          +BY week DESC LIMIT 1").fetchone()                             
      457 +        return _weekly_example_row(r) if r else None          
      458 +                                                              
      459 +                                                              
      460 +def archive_weekly_examples() -> list[dict]:                  
      461 +    """All auto examples EXCEPT the featured latest, newest fi
          +rst — the browsable archive."""                               
      462 +    with connect() as conn:                                   
      463 +        conn.execute(_WEEKLY_EXAMPLES_DDL)                    
      464 +        rows = conn.execute("SELECT * FROM weekly_examples ORD
          +ER BY week DESC").fetchall()                                  
      465 +        return [_weekly_example_row(r) for r in rows[1:]]     
      466 +                                                              
      467 +                                                              
      468  def run_kind(label: str) -> Optional[str]:
      469      """'weekly' | 'backfill' | None — lets the release page ti
           tle itself 'Release month' vs
      470      'Release week' for a given label."""

⏺ All four parts are done and live on the site.

  1 — New intro

  Replaced the old sentence with a 3-sentence summary of what's on the page
  (weekly auto-catch + curated deep-dives, with the deviation-colour key).

  2 — Key facts under the KPIs

  Key-facts panel now sits in a shared right-hand column (.exside) directly
  beneath the KPI tiles — viewer and KPI grid keep their sizes; key facts
  resized to fit that column. The narrative sections went full-width.

  3 — Collapsible "Read more" sections

  All narrative sections are native <details>, collapsed by default, with a
  "Read more ▾ / Show less ▴" toggle.

  4 — Auto "example of the week" + full archive (built + generating all weeks)

  I built the templated, no-LLM pipeline exactly as you specified:

  - Biology removed from the auto class — auto panels have only Structure & 
  architecture and Why AlphaFold gets it wrong (verified: featured = 2 sections,
  curated = 3 with Biology). The biology is the one thing a template can't
  honestly write, so it's left to the curated deep-dives.
  - "🎣 Fresh catch — example of the week {date}" section at the top. Featured
  now: RNA Pol II subunit GRINL1A (9T5H, released 2026-07-15) — pLDDT 88.5, TM
  0.42, 96% confidently wrong, 100% novel. A clean "right fold, wrong
  conformation" catch.
  - "🗄️ Weekly archive — 345 previous catches" panel below it: a compact,
  filterable list (search by protein/UniProt/week/failure-mode), each row
  natively expandable to its full templated panel with a link to the entry's
  full analysis.
  - Generated all previous weeks: one catch per PDB release date, best
  non-curated CW structure per week (novel-first, then FRAUD), the 8 curated
  families excluded so nothing duplicates. 346 weeks total → 1 featured + 345
  archived.
  - Runs after each weekly update: pipeline.run() now calls
  db.rebuild_weekly_examples() after the analysis refresh, so every Wednesday
  the fresh catch and archive update themselves. There's also a manual
  AlphaFraud.py weekly-examples command.

  New/changed pieces: alphafraud/examples_auto.py (the deterministic generator),
  a weekly_examples table + rebuild/accessors in db.py, a reusable
  _expanel.html partial (featured + curated share markup), the archive UI in
  examples.html + brand.css, and an archive filter in app.js. Deployed as
  43a8041, home cache cleared.

  One thing worth flagging: the live weekly runs haven't caught a
  confidently-wrong structure yet (all catches so far are from the historical
  backfill), so the featured date tracks the most recent release week with a 
  catch. Once the Wednesday watch lands its first CW catch, that week
  automatically becomes the fresh catch.

  Want me to add this feature to the AlphaFraud README/roadmap, or leave the
  docs until the weekly watch produces its first live catch?

  ├─────────────┼────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Cα-RMSD     │ 0.45 Å │ sub-Ångström, on a 1.5 Å crystal         │
  ├─────────────┼────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ mean pLDDT  │ 95.2   │ high confidence…                         │
  ├─────────────┼────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ FRAUD score │ 0.02   │ …that AlphaFraud certifies as earned     │
  ├─────────────┼────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ novelty     │ 71%    │ only 29% identity to anything pre-cutoff │
  └─────────────┴────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────┘

  The story it tells is exactly what you asked for: AlphaFold predicted a
  disease-critical DNA-repair nuclease it had never seen (deposited 2020, 71%
  novel) almost perfectly, and AlphaFraud's pipeline gives it a near-zero fraud 
  score — demonstrating both that AlphaFold can be brilliantly accurate and that
  the analysis isn't rigged to find failures everywhere (worst offenders score
  0.8–0.95; this scores 0.02).

  How it's presented distinctly

  - Its own green section, "When AlphaFold gets it right — the control", with a
  section note explaining the purpose.
  - Green (not red) styling: a green "Excellent match — AlphaFold nailed it"
  chip, five green KPI tiles (TM/lDDT/pLDDT/RMSD/FRAUD), green headline accent.
  - The middle section is retitled "✅ Why AlphaFold gets it right" and is
  expanded by default (the other panels start collapsed) so the positive
  analysis is the first thing readers see.
  - Its Cα-deviation ribbon renders essentially all-blue (agreement), visually
  reinforcing the match.
  - Intro rewritten to lead with the balance message: "AlphaFraud hunts for
  confident failures, but it is an even-handed analyzer — so we open with a
  positive control…"

  The page now has 10 panels: the Artemis control, the auto fresh catch, and the
  8 curated failures.

  nav doesn't cover it, and a blue flash (:target) when you land so the right
  panel is obvious.

⏺ All three issues fixed and verified live (08a4312):

  What changed

  1. Original circle markers, not stars — the highlight trace now uses symbol: 
  circle (size 15) matching the cloud style, colored red (#d62728) for the 8
  failures and green (#0a8a4f) for Artemis, with a white ring so they pop.

  2. Pulse + grow for all users — removed the prefers-reduced-motion guard; the
  markers now continuously pulse ~12→24 px for everyone (still freezing briefly
  on hover so the tooltip stays readable).

  3. Links now go to the Examples tab, not /entry — this was the important bug.
  The 9 examples were being drawn on top of their original cloud points, so a
  click resolved to the buried original (→ /entry/…). Now:
  - The 9 examples are pulled out of the main cloud entirely (verified: 0 
  duplicate example points remain in the underlying traces), so there's nothing
  underneath to catch the click.
  - Clicks route by an entity-id → link map (el._exLinks) built from the trace,
  so a click on any example marker goes to /examples#ex-<id> regardless of which
  trace Plotly reports. Non-example points (like the 6H6T_1 you hit) still
  correctly go to /entry.

  The hover tooltip still shows the protein name + failure mode + "▶ click to
  open the deep-dive", the ribbon preview still appears, and landing on a panel
  still flashes it blue.

  Home cache cleared, so it's live now — the red/green circles should be pulsing
  in the scatter and clicking one jumps to its deep-dive on the Examples tab.

      135  ## 🧮 Coverage and skipped entities
      136  
      137  Not every deposited entity can be compared. AlphaFold DB holds
            a single model per human UniProt sequence (monomers only), so
            anything that does not map cleanly to one accession, has no m
           odel covering its resolved range, or is too small or too large
            to compare is recorded as *skipped* rather than forced throug
           h.

      247  ## 🧮 Coverage and skipped entities
      248  
      249  Not every deposited entity can be compared. AlphaFold DB holds
            a single model per human UniProt sequence (monomers only), so
            anything that does not map cleanly to one accession, has no m
           odel covering its resolved range, or is too small or too large
            to compare is recorded as *skipped* rather than forced throug
           h.

  What changed in the README

  1) Live-site links throughout (34 in total):
  - A "The site is live at alphafraud.mdeller.com" line above the routes table,
  and each concrete route (/, /leaderboard, /analysis, /examples, /archive) now
  links to the live page.
  - In the Examples section prose, every protein and feature is linked to its
  live target — Artemis, the auto example-of-the-week, the weekly archive, all 8
  failure panels, and the home fraud-quadrant scatter.

  2) A subsection per example — the existing Examples section is untouched, and
  below it all 9 examples now have a ### subsection (🟢 for the Artemis control,
  🔴 for the 8 failures), each with the same unified table:

  ┌─────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │           Row           │                    Content                    │
  ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Take-home               │ one-line message                              │
  ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ KPIs                    │ TM · lDDT · pLDDT · Cα-RMSD · FRAUD · novelty │
  │                         │  (exact live values)                          │
  ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Stats                   │ depositions ×N, confidently-wrong count,      │
  │                         │ calibration (pLDDT↔lDDT)                      │
  ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Structure               │ fold/architecture in a sentence               │
  ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Why AlphaFold gets it   │ the mechanism                                 │
  │ right/wrong             │                                               │
  ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Biology & disease       │ function + disease link                       │
  ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Key fact                │ the single most telling detail                │
  └─────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Each subsection title links straight to its live interactive panel, and I
  added a one-line metric legend so a reader knows which way is "good" for each
  number. Numbers were pulled fresh from the live database so they match the
  site exactly.

  Both forged HTML sets (README.* and repo-named AlphaFraud.*) plus the widget
  CSS were regenerated via the marcs-vibe-coding forge and are in the commit.

  ⎿  · You want the input to autocomplete only to entries meeting all criteria.
